Quote: Eldrial, Dec 3 2005, 03:26 AM
hah, thanks.  but if you see any other errors, let me know.  some revising will do it good.  i want it to be the best it can be.
For Mac OS X, instead of

/Users/Short name/Library/Preferences/......

It can just be:

~/Library/Preferences/......

It's the same thing, and it won't confuse anyone (I hope.) For all Mac users out there who don't know what this is, '~/' is your user folder, and '/' is your hard drive.

#142622 2 2006-02-12T16:16:43Z jtbandes
Edit: oops, didn't see whaleshow's post, however this way might be easier.

Okay, here is the way I found when I got to that level (neither description nor picture will be as good as those made by Aries ). The points I talk about in detail should have dots in the picture. A dot also means that you need to wait there so as not to get killed. The route is in black until you get the switch, where it turns blue.


As soon as the level starts, jump over the waves. Bounce off of the last one into the air a bit, then fall back down on the convex side of it and use that momentum to propel you up the right wall. Land at the door, get the gold if you want, then wait for the area below to be clear and fall down to the lowermost ledge. Note that if you jump off of the door platform to get to the lower ledge, you WILL die if you don't slide down the wall to the left of it.

Once you get down to the ledge alive, wait for the drone in front of you (to your left) to go down, then slide slowly down the tiny wall to your left. You will most likely need to walljump once so you stay on that wall longer. Then, fall down the angled slope below you, and get into that small dent in the floor before the drone gets there (it will pass over you). Quickly jump to the switch.

After you've gotten the switch, walljump up the passage above you. Hold down the jump key at the end, because you'll need to stay up above the drone until it passes. Fall down behind the drone, then walljump up the long passage on the left. Quickly come out the top ledge, and jump to the door.

#165248 357 2006-04-16T02:43:19Z jtbandes
INTERNET EXPLORER FOR MAC NO LONGER AVAILABLE FOR DOWNLOAD

In June 2003, the Microsoft Macintosh Business Unit announced that Internet Explorer for Mac would undergo no further development, and support would cease in 2005. In accordance with published support lifecycle policies, Microsoft ended support for Internet Explorer for Mac on December 31st, 2005, and is not providing any further security or performance updates.

Accordingly, as of January 31st, 2006, Internet Explorer for the Mac is no longer available for download from Microsoft. It is recommended that Macintosh users migrate to more recent web browsing technologies such as Apple's Safari.

#178621 31 2006-05-21T15:20:19Z jtbandes
I've beaten the game too. Here are some tips:
(You can please quote me on these if other people ask for help)

Jtbandes's 10 Tips for Beating N:
1. Breathe.
2. Breathe in AND out.
3. Try not to get frustrated or angry. If you do, take a break. Sometimes even a break as long as a day or more.
4. Watch the highscores. They have good routes. If some of the higher ones are over your head, watch the last few highscores.
5. If you feel you are really stuck on a level, pick a different column and go on. I think many people, including myself, skipped to the 90s when they hit MTI (88-4). Come back to the other level later.
6. HAVE FUN! Really, that's the whole point of a game, not to get mad at it. Learn to have fun while beating levels.
7. Practice, practice, practice. I often am seen picking a random episode that I already beat and playing it again to practice. Even the first column.
8. While playing a difficult level, devise a route that works as you go. Follow that route the same way each time, and each time you will get a little further and add on to your route. This also work with a fixed route, when you can devise a technique for each part of the route.
9. Believe in yourself. I don't consider myself an extremely good player, but I still managed to beat the game. If I can, so can you.
10. Don't give up!

Good luck!

Quote: viper093, June 10, 2006 07:01 am
God I must suck. I can't even get the pink one

In fact, I only beat 10 columns of the whole game, so yeah, I suck.
Will you stop SAYING THAT?!
It takes practice to beat columns. If you are stuck on any particular level, don't hesitate to post about it in Level/Ned help. Just don't give up. Do you think the people who have beaten the game gave up when they got to hard levels? No. Should you? Definitely not! So, if you're not quite as good as them, just think: when you beat the game, it will be a bigger accomplishment than when they did! Some of them are the people who can just whip out a 0th high score, and if you are trying to compare yourself to them, or anyone who's farther than you are, you can stop now, because it won't work. You're putting it in perspective, but with a negative effect. Try looking at it and thinking than they were probably at the same situation as you, but they kept trying. If you practice and have confidence, you will end up as good as they are. Keep trying and good luck!
[/offtopic]

what do u do? Like put 3 bounce pads in 1 place and 3 bounce pads in another?

Nope, you just modify one or both of the last two numbers in the code for the launchpad. Depending on how you do it, this can give you a launchpad that launches you, but only a few pixels, sticking you to it; a launchpad that is simply more powerful than normal; or a teleporter, which is really just a launchpad that makes you go so fast that you are in a completely different place in the next frame.

But this is no place for a teleporter discussion, there are already too many of those threads. Just post in the tutorial thread linked in an above post if you have any questions.

And yes, cheating death happens when you hit an enemy and a launchpad in the EXACT SAME frame.

Quote: iangb, July 06, 2006 01:27 am
No, time really does slow down. And not just because it looks that way.

Real-world example: 2 atomic clocks were set up in perfect synchrony. One stayed still, while the other was put onto a plane and flown around the world. When the two clocks were compared after the travelling clock had returned, the stationary clock was ahead of the travelling one. Only by fractions of miliseconds, but the difference was there.

Yeah, relativity is wierd. At the speed of light: you have infinite mass, 0 length, and time does not pass for you.

I just don't understand what actually slows time down.

#201332 67 2006-07-11T17:06:46Z jtbandes
My thoughts:

TNG
Nothing much to say, besides that I can't wait and I will definetly pay every penny of it when it comes out! Take your sweet time and don't kill yourselves over it!

N v1.5
  • Highscores - I think adding extra security and locking the old scores is a great choice. The one thing I'm worried about is how this will happen. Will you encrypt the .sol file? Because one time, some of my highscores on userlevels showed up as exactly 90.000, which was incorrect. I had to manually go into the .sol and muck with it so as to change them to 0.000 so I could redo the highscore run and get the correct numbers back. How you decide to secure the highscores is up to you guys (obviously), but I just want to still have the ability to fix my highscores if they get messed up. Or you could investigate the problem and make sure it can't happen again.
  • Slope stuttering - Even if Atilla and I are the only ones who know what you're talking about, please go ahead and fix it! It might make a couple angle jumping levels easier than intended, such as Twisted, but I say too bad for the mapmakers. Make harder maps.
  • True surface-follow drones - I love this idea, but only as an option in Ned. This way the mapmaker can choose whether the drones do the 90 degree thing, or actually follow all surfaces. It's nice to be able to slip by a drone by going into a curved or sloped area.
  • Chainguns - I think the only thing that should change here is the randomness. The line of fire needs to be predictable, and I think you should do that, while leaving the ability to dodge the bullets. This would make the dodging mostly skill and little or no luck.
  • Corner jumping - Please, leave it as it is.

Overall, do whatever you like for TNG, I can wait, and I'm sure it will be great! For N, keep most physics-type things the same, please, and whatever you do, have the option to use the old physics, but in a way similar to the current Practice Mode. More levels! More flavours! More secrets! You guys are awesome! Keep up the good work!

#202041 3 2006-07-13T04:41:09Z jtbandes
Well, my thought is that it would actually be easy to implement. But first, here's some clarification:

Let's say you have three userlevels files: one for the ones that come with the game, one for your favorites, and one for the Legacy levels. Now, each time you wanted to switch between these, you have to rename them. With my idea, going to the userlevels menu option in the game would give you a list with those three files in it: Original userlevels, Favorites, Legacy. From there, you'd click on one of the collections to load those levels, where you would see a screen like the one that is currently implemented in the userlevels.

I'd think that, since the game can already parse the userlevels.txt, it would be easy to do. The game would just look at any .txt files in its directory, and see which ones were userlevels collections (which ones have the "&userdata=" line). It would then grab the name of each collection out of a user-specified line (maybe, for instance, instead of "&userdata=," "&usercollection=Legacy&userdata="), and you'd see an easy-to-understand list of your collections.

A student at a school of music must turn in a composition for his final. So, instead of composing something himself, he goes to the school library and finds his teacher's old final. #206903 12 2006-07-25T00:19:49Z jtbandes
Yeah, blargh, I remember. So, here are the movements.

Edit: first post edited to include wedgie.

Note: All clockwise/counterclockwise movements are to be used as if you were looking straight at the face in question. References to the faces are used as if the front (F) face were towards you.

Faces
  • U - The top face of the cube.
  • D - The bottom face.
  • L - The left face.
  • R - The right face.
  • F - The front face.
  • B - The back face.
Movements
  • Movements are shown by the face to be rotated. A letter on its own (such as D) is a clockwise turn of the face, and a letter with an apostrophe after it is a counterclockwise turn (such as D'). A turn of 180 is shown by a 2 before the face (such as 2B).
  • For example: 2F R' D' R means: Rotate the front face 180, the right face counterclockwise, the bottom face counterclockwise, and the right face clockwise.
  • If you need to undo a move, change clockwises to counterclockwises and vice versa, then go through the sequence of moves backwards.

#215320 2 2006-08-14T03:25:54Z jtbandes
Which type of door?

Normal doors [i]: These are tricky. It mostly takes tons of experimenting, but the main idea is to have some gold on the door. The ninja goes towards the door, and it opens, but when the ninja starts to collect the gold, the door tries to close on top of him, propelling him away.

Trapdoors [p]: Much easier. All you do is place the switch so that the ninja closes the door when he's still slightly inside it.

I don't think it's possible with locked doors [o]...?

So, today I had my friend over to play around on the computer. He brought his laptop. We have a wireless router, so he needed the encryption key. I called my mom to see if that was okay, and she said she'd call me back after she talked with her girlfriend (who owns the house). I got a call back, saying that he could access the router as long as I put the key in for him, and he didn't see it, and also I would watch him delete it before he left. VERY important. I put the key in for him, as asked.

During the course of the next few hours, he randomly decided to wipe his hard drive and install the latest Windows Vista beta (instead of the former XP). But before he did this, I watched him delete the encryption key. So my brain registered seeing him delete it. Of course, I had to re-enter it for him once Vista was up and running. A bit confusing, no?

So... my mom gets home. We move some furniture that she brought, and then get ready to leave and take my friend back to his house (and me to my other house). My mom asks me if I saw him delete the key. I stutter "yes... no." I was confused because of his reinstall. But while I'm in limbo between saying yes and no, my friend starts to say no, being the honest person he is. My mom takes this as me only saying no because he did, and after the fact remembering why he said no. My mom gets mad at me.

So then we drop him off at his house, and on the way to my other house, I get a huge lecture about how what I did was unacceptable and betrayed her trust, and that the next time I'm at her house, we'll talk about consequences, and that I should come up with consequences, otherwise, it's her decision, which it ultimately is anyway (yes, she said that). Essentially blows up at me. And ignores or denies all of my attempts to say that I slipped because of the Windows reinstall, and my attempts to explain all of that. (Note that she had explained that the entire ordeal of having him over when we're the only ones there is a sort of 'test' to see if I'll be allowed to do this in the future.)

Adding to this is the fact that I'm also jealous because my friend is going to be learning to drive soon. This applies to all my friends from school, who are all older than me, and either are or will be turning 15, while I sit around, waiting to turn 14 in 5 months.



Please give advice, or not, but typing this helped me.

It's not a bug. This happens when you beat a level in the normal game, with the time that carried over from levels earlier in the episode. The reason it's ---.--- is that had you started with 90 seconds, you would not have beaten the level. The times in the highscores menu are based on starting each level with 90 seconds.

#11315 1 2006-09-19T03:16:28Z jtbandes
I documented the individual steps to making that banner, so it's a kind of tutorial.

Note: I had this screenshot for a reference:


Step 1: Start with a gradient background.


Step 2: Make a tree (like they are in the game) out of triangles.


Step 3: Copy some trees, make them different sizes and heights.


Step 4: Make a whole foreground of trees this way.


Step 5: Add another, different, lighter layer of trees behind the first.


Step 6: Add a third layer.


Final: Add subtle gradient on top, text with elliptical gradient, signature. Done!

#230896 18 2006-09-19T23:19:30Z jtbandes
Quote: Cerberus, September 19, 2006 04:01 pm
I have nothing against Macs, but the desktops don't have right clicks, right?

(Correct me if I'm wrong or if it was posted already but I'm too lazy to read the longer posts)

They do now, didn't used to.

I was raised with both Macs and PCs, so I'm very good with both of them. Naturally, I formed my own opinions that Macs are better, but I think that's because I'm more creative than businessy. PCs definitely win on the business level, and they have more applications made for them. But as Mr. Mac Dude in the ads said, Macs just kinda "ffffffftt"; they are comprised of applications that work extremely well together. And it all looks good, too. PCs are better for gaming because there are more PC games than Mac. Macs are better for gaming because of their graphics. PCs are currently made a bit faster than Macs, but that could be changing. #238410 9 2006-10-08T13:43:50Z jtbandes
I have conducted a test, and concluded that the drone's distance from the door has nothing to do with whether this happens or not. If the drone sees you when the door is open, it will go through, regardless of which side of the door you were on. If the drone sees you when the door is closed, it will only go through if you are "holding" the door open for it (i.e. you are standing on the other side). In this case, it will charge through the open door and rearrange your limbs a bit. If the door was closed when it saw you and it remains closed, the drone will not go through.

#256717 94 2006-11-19T02:17:45Z jtbandes
Quote: Monkeyfeces Nutmonger III, November 18, 2006 10:37 am
Now presenting: The Sig Request From Hell!

I'm not sure if I'm allowed to do this one, because it specifies things beyond the criteria you
normally fill out. Ah well. It's not as scary as it looks.

Slaps and I are halves, and therefore, we must have matching sigs. They are very similar, so you can skim the second description, but important differences are bolded.

BIGBLARGH'S SIG

There is a room. You can see the top right corner of it, where the brick walls meet the cement ceiling. There is an lightbulb that is flickering (animated) with a red glow, illuminating the room and a piece of graffiti in the middle of the wall that proclaims "BIGBLARGH" in red rounded bubble letters (meaning curvy letters with white outside, kinda like eggs). The wall itself should have cracks, missing bricks, and maybe a cobweb. Make sure the cobweb casts a shadow if it's in the right place. Try to animate the sig with the light more often off than on.

SLAPPYMCGEE's SIG

There is a room. You can see the top left corner of it, where the brick walls meet the cement ceiling. There is an lightbulb that is flickering (animated) with a blue glow, illuminating the room and a piece of graffiti in the middle of the wall that proclaims "SLAPPYMCGEE" in blue pointed lightning-like bubble letters (meaning curvy letters with white outside, kinda like eggs). The wall itself should have cracks, missing bricks, and maybe a cobweb. Make sure the cobweb casts a shadow if it's in the right place. Try to animate the sig with the light more often off than on.

Primary colours: A dark brick color, as the room is made of bricks.
Theme: Hmm... grunge, I suppose.
Renders/images: A lightbulb, graffiti, maybe a cobweb.
Complexity: Perhaps not complex, but very specific.
Matching avatar (yes or no): No thanks.
Dimensions (size): Anything sane.
Preferred artist (optional): The artist doesn't matter, but I wrote this with Red Reamer in mind because I would love to see this with his style.

Take as long as you want.



You know what I'm talking about.

#12974 1 2006-12-18T23:05:04Z jtbandes
Post your ninja images from N or N Poser here, and I'll make them transparent so you can easily use them on all backgrounds!

Example:
becomes
becomes

My hope is that this will be very useful for people making webcomics or sigs/sigtags, where a transparent background is very helpful for them.

If you have a plain black ninja:
  • Simply post the image of it from N or N poser.
If you have a colored ninja:
  • Post the same pose with a black ninja. ALSO:
  • Give me the color code for the color, or a screenshot of the color.
I'm working on an easier way to do the colored ones, but for now, this is it.

#273906 2 2006-12-20T18:11:38Z jtbandes
For each given episode (in this case episode 40), you have 90 seconds to complete that entire episode. What happened for you is that you used almost all of your time on 40-0 and 40-1, so there's none left for 40-2. The only way around this is to play episode 40 again, making sure to get gold (your timer gains 2 seconds per piece) and not to waste time.

#277443 41 2006-12-26T19:08:24Z jtbandes
Quote: Tsukatu, December 24, 2006 01:07 pm
A simpler one for those of you who can't figure out the above:
You have two lengths of fuse.
Each fuse takes exactly one hour to burn completely.
Each fuse is threaded together out of dozens of smaller bits of fuse, each of which burn at a different speed, so it is impossible to tell how long the fuse has been burning based on the length of fuse expended so far (e.g. if the fuse has burned halfway, that does not necessarily mean that it has been half an hour).
You have these two fuses and a lighter.

Time 45 minutes exactly.
Light both ends of Fuse A and one end of Fuse B. When Fuse A burns out, light the other end of Fuse B. Fuse B will have burned out in 45 minutes.


As for the troll thing, maybe you could go partway to the opposite edge and then turn 90 degrees? I'm not going to bother testing that.
Edit: yeah, lord_day's probably right.

#277478 49 2006-12-26T21:29:31Z jtbandes
If one person has blue eyes, it is obviously them, since nobody else has blue eyes.

If 2 people have blue eyes, neither of them will leave on day 1 because they think the other person will. On day 2, when neither of them leave, they realize that the other must be seeing at least one blue-eyed person, and since they both see only one, that person must be them. They both leave.

If 3 people have blue eyes, none of them will leave on day 1 because they are thinking that the others will do what the 2 people did in case 2. When this does not happen, they all realize that the others must see at least 2 other blue-eyed people, or they would have left already. Since they only see one other, it must be them. They all leave.

And so on.

The blue-eyed people all leave on day 100.

1) You have 9 gold coins. One is a fake and is lighter than the others. You cannot tell which one is the fake one by holding it, and all you have to use is this scale:



Only using it three times, work out which coin is fake.

Weigh 6 of the coins in two groups of three. If one is lighter, use that for the next step. If they weigh the same, use the pile you didn't weigh. Weigh two of those coins against each other. If one is lighter, you have the fake coin; if they're the same weight, the leftover coin is fake.

#281866 7 2007-01-06T06:17:52Z jtbandes
The way I do gravity in all the mini pointless games I make is to have an xvel and a yvel variable, and a gravity constant defined at the beginning. Each frame, if the up key is not held (or in N's case, if jumping has run out of time), add gravity (because positive is down) from the yvel. This way, you accelerate downwards. Don't forget to add xvel and yvel to your character's position each frame.

#13514 1 2007-01-14T16:47:26Z jtbandes
(I wasn't sure if I should put this in Images, if you think it'd be better there, please move it.)
This is a really cool (and free!) fractal program I found: Context Free.

You can either play with examples or learn the language, which is not too hard; here's a little info:

You have to start with 
startshape flower
or whatever the initial shape to draw is. You define shapes (rules) this way:
startshape flower

rule flower {
	// code goes here
}
All shapes are basically made of squares, triangles, and circles. You make those like this:
startshape flower

rule flower {
	SQUARE{}
	TRIANGLE{}
	CIRCLE{}
}
When you draw shapes, you can give them properties. For example, this makes a square rotated 45 degrees:
startshape flower

rule flower {
	SQUARE{r 45}
}
and this makes the same square, 3 times bigger:
startshape flower

rule flower {
	SQUARE{r 45 s 3}
}

So now you see the basic way of modifying the simple shapes. These are some of the properties you can use:
y: shifts shape on the y-axis
x: shifts shape on the x-axis
s: size
r: rotation
hue: hue, from 0-255
sat: saturation, from 0-1
b: brightness, from 0-1
a: alpha (transparency)
skew: to skew a shape, can take 1 or 2 parameters ("skew 10" or "skew 10 25" or something like that)
flip: flips the shape across a line positioned at the number of degrees specified

Now, suppose you want to draw more than just one shape; let's say a tower of squares that shrinks as it gets taller. Well, to do that, you'd write:
startshape flower

rule flower {
	SQUARE{}
	flower{y 1 s 0.99}
}
This will draw a square, then execute the flower rule again, but shifted up one unit and at 99% of the size. Context Free only renders shapes until they are too small to see. This means that it will not render the above code forever, only until it gets small enough to be invisible.

How about curves? Just add an "r 1" after "y 1 s 0.99" in the above adjustments for the flower rule.

Of course, if you want to make a tree, you're not just going to have a single line. You'd want branches, right? But how would you make them besides randomness?
startshape flower

rule flower 1 {
	SQUARE{}
	flower{y 1 s 0.99}
}
rule flower 0.1 {
	SQUARE{}
	flower{y 1 s 0.99}
	flower{y 1 s 0.5 r 45}
}
Notice the addition of numbers after the name of the rule you're defining ("rule flower 1" and "rule flower 0.1"): these set the probability for each version of a rule. In this case, the second definition of flower is 10 times less likely to be used than the first. But each time it's called, that rule will do one of those two things. Another thing to notice is that the second definition draws a flower twice: once to continue the original "trunk" of the tree, and the other for a branch. So, if you render the above, you'd get something to the effect of:


Oh, noes! We only have half a tree! Why is this? Well, because we never defined a rule for the tree to go the other direction. Let's.
startshape flower

rule flower 1 {
	SQUARE{}
	flower{y 1 s 0.99}
}
rule flower 0.1 {
	SQUARE{}
	flower{y 1 s 0.99}
	flower{y 1 s 0.5 r 45}
}
rule flower 0.1 {
	SQUARE{}
	flower{y 1 s 0.99}
	flower{y 1 s 0.5 r -45}
}
Now we've added a third rule just as likely as the second one, but it rotates the other direction. Let's render:

Whee, a tree!

Now, go download Context Free and render this:
startshape init

rule init{
	tree{}
}

rule tree 1{
	SQUARE{}
	tree {y 1 s .99 r 1}
}

rule tree .01 {
	SQUARE{}
	tree {y 1 s .99 r 1}
	tree {y 1 s .99 flip 90}
}

rule tree .01 {
	SQUARE{}
	tree {y 1 s .99 r 1}
	tree {y 1 s .6}
}
You'll get a very special swirly tree!

#291837 3 2007-01-30T20:55:15Z jtbandes
Most likely the cause of your problem is that you haven't copied N and its files out of the disk image. Your best option is to copy all the files out of that folder onto your hard drive. Personally, I made a folder called "N v1.4" in my Applications folder, and put all the files in there. Once you've copied the files, you can eject the disk image by dragging it to the trash, and you can also get rid of the n_v1mac.dmg file. At that point, you can just play N right off your hard drive!

What I like to do is have a timer instead of doing it each frame:


var frametimer = setInterval(frame, 10);

function frame() {
    if (Key.isDown(Key.LEFT)) {
        stick._x--;
    } else if (Key.isDown(Key.RIGHT)) {
        stick._x++;
    }
    
    updateAfterEvent();
}

#306098 5 2007-03-18T16:11:38Z jtbandes
Quote: Toshosoft, March 17, 2007 09:30 am
You could try setInterval() if you're really picky about the rate. You just make a function which checks for key-presses and call it every I-don't-know-how-many milliseconds. Check it up in the reference.
That's what I do, because it seems like the best way to have control over the game's performance. Take note, however, that if you leave the framerate at 12 with this technique, it'll still be a little jerky. So I set the framerate to 30. And now for a short guide on how to do what TS said.

(All this is the Actionscript for the first frame. You should only need one frame in most cases.)

Create a timer that runs the frame() function every several milliseconds (I use 10).
var frametimer = setInterval(frame, 10);
Then, make the frame() function.
function frame() {
  if (Key.isDown(Key.UP)) {
    _root.you._y--;
  } else if (Key.isDown(Key.DOWN)) {
    _root.you._y++;
  }
  
  if (Key.isDown(Key.LEFT)) {
    _root.you._x--;
  } else if (Key.isDown(Key.RIGHT)) {
    _root.you._x++;
  }
  
  updateAfterEvent();
}
That's a very simple function to take the keyboard input and move around an object with the instance name "you". The updateAfterEvent(); refreshes the screen, so each time this function is run (every 10ms or so, depending on your computer's speed), the screen is redrawn and you get smooth movement.

#14721 1 2007-04-05T01:02:09Z jtbandes
function SpockyMenu(id, opts) {
    _root.attachMovie("menu", id, 0);
    this.mc = eval("_root."+id);
    this.options = new Array();
    for (var i = 0; i < opts.length; i++) {
        this.mc.attachMovie("menuitem", "item"+i, i, {_x:70, _y:32*i});
        eval("this.mc.item"+i).displaylabel.text = opts[i];
        options[i] = eval("this.mc.item"+i);
    }
    this.mc.attachMovie("spocky", "spocky", i+1);
    this.spocky = mc.spocky;
    this.spockypos = {_x:0, _y:0};
    this.spockytimer = setInterval(_root.move, 10, this.spocky, this.spockypos, 10);
    this.curropt = 0;
    this.keylistener = new Object();
    this.keylistener.onKeyDown = function() {
        if (Key.getCode() == Key.DOWN) {
            if (this.curropt < this.options.length - 1) {
                this.spockypos._y = this.options[this.curropt + 1]._y;
                this.curropt++;
            } else {
                this.spockypos._y = this.options[0]._y;
                this.curropt = 0;
            }
        } else if (Key.getCode() == Key.UP) {
            if (this.curropt > 0) {
                this.spockypos._y = this.options[this.curropt - 1]._y;
                this.curropt--;
            } else {
                this.spockypos._y = this.options[this.options.length - 1]._y;
                this.curropt = this.options.length - 1;
            }
        }
    };
    Key.addListener(this.keylistener);
}

And, of course, 
var testmenu = new SpockyMenu("test", ["test1","test2","test3"]);
to make a menu.

What does work is to take off every instance of "this." in the code, but I don't want to do that, because that makes everything public. Anybody know how to fix it?

  if (Key.getCode() == Key.DOWN) {
    if (this.parent.curropt < this.parent.options.length - 1) {
      this.parent.spockypos._y = this.parent.options[this.parent.curropt + 1]._y;
      this.parent.curropt++;

#312932 14 2007-04-14T00:46:56Z jtbandes
*resurrect*

I was bored, and poked around Fan Programs to find something to port to Mac.
Here it is, Tile Inverter made into a Mac app with Applescript!

Icon:

Spoiler: In case you're interested, here's the code.
-- Initial variables
set mapdata to ""
set authordata to ""
set objectdata to ""

-- A small function I found on the internet to replace text in a string
to searchReplace(thisText, searchTerm, replacement)
    set AppleScript's text item delimiters to searchTerm
    set thisText to thisText's text items
    set AppleScript's text item delimiters to replacement
    set thisText to "" & thisText
    set AppleScript's text item delimiters to {""}
    return thisText
end searchReplace

-- A function that swaps all instances of one char with another...
-- Useful for inverting tilesets
to swapChars(thisText, char1, char2)
    set thisText to searchReplace(thisText, char1, "X")
    set thisText to searchReplace(thisText, char2, char1)
    set thisText to searchReplace(thisText, "X", char2)
    return thisText
end swapChars

-- It's a good idea to actually figure out what we're inverting
display dialog "Paste map data here:" default answer mapdata
set mapdata to text returned of the result

-- Chop the author info ($Title#Author#Category#) off the data (and store it), if it exists
set hashPos to offset of "#" in mapdata
repeat while (hashPos is not equal to length of mapdata) and (hashPos is not equal to 0)
    set authordata to authordata & text 1 thru hashPos of mapdata
    set mapdata to text (hashPos + 1) thru (length of mapdata) of mapdata
    set hashPos to offset of "#" in mapdata
end repeat

-- Chop the object info (|...) off the data (and store it), if it exists
set pipePos to offset of "|" in mapdata
if pipePos is not equal to 0 then
    set objectdata to text pipePos thru (length of mapdata) of mapdata
    set mapdata to text 1 thru (pipePos - 1) of mapdata
end if

-- Invert!
set mapdata to swapChars(mapdata, "1", "0")
set mapdata to swapChars(mapdata, "2", "4")
set mapdata to swapChars(mapdata, "3", "5")
set mapdata to swapChars(mapdata, "F", "L")
set mapdata to swapChars(mapdata, "G", "M")
set mapdata to swapChars(mapdata, "H", "J")
set mapdata to swapChars(mapdata, "I", "K")
set mapdata to swapChars(mapdata, ">", "D")
set mapdata to swapChars(mapdata, "?", "E")
set mapdata to swapChars(mapdata, "@", "B")
set mapdata to swapChars(mapdata, "A", "C")
set mapdata to swapChars(mapdata, "6", "<")
set mapdata to swapChars(mapdata, "9", ";")
set mapdata to swapChars(mapdata, "8", ":")
set mapdata to swapChars(mapdata, "7", "=")
set mapdata to swapChars(mapdata, "Q", "O")
set mapdata to swapChars(mapdata, "N", "P")

-- Let's not be greedy and keep it all to ourselves.
set mapdata to authordata & mapdata & objectdata
display dialog "Inverted map data:" default answer mapdata 
    buttons ["Copy", "OK"] default button 1
-- Provide a nice way to copy it to the clipboard.
if button returned of the result is "Copy" then
    set the clipboard to mapdata
end if

#15218 1 2007-05-13T20:43:58Z jtbandes
For basic Mafia rules, see http://www.mafiascum.net/cgi-bin/wiki.pl?Newbie_Guide.

Deathy Mafia is a variation of Mafia in which there are five players: four cops and a mafia. Each cop is of a different type. The four types of cops are:
- Sane: Always gets the correct result of investigations.
- Insane: Always gets the incorrect result.
- Nave: Always gets "innocent" as a result.
- Paranoid: Always gets "guilty."
If you are a cop, you are not told which type you are. You must deduce this by reasoning and logic, and decide who the mafia is with that information. During the day, players may say what results they got on their investigation. In the case of the mafia, they may lie and make up a result.

At the beginning of the game, the moderator tells everyone their roles, cop or mafia. All the cops get a head start, and may investigate someone immediately. The mafia must wait until the first night to make a kill.

#17103 1 2007-09-06T14:15:24Z jtbandes
This script allows you to define macros for commands. For example, I might type:

/define k kick %0-

And then I could simply do:

/k nick reason

to kick someone. Or, for a simple "fish" script,

/define fish me slaps %0 around a bit with a fish %1-

/fish jtbandes because he feels like it.

And that would send:

/me slaps jtbandes around a bit with a fish because he feels like it.

Get it?

http://pastebin.com/f1d191b08

Take that and save it as "define.pl", then load it as a script in XChat.
Note to windows users: if the script does not work, as in it says something about register(), delete every instance of "Xchat::" (without quotes) in the file.

From the help:
Usage: DEFINE [-t] <cmd1> <cmd2> [<format> ...], defines cmd1 as a macro for cmd2.
    The -t option creates a temporary command that will be destroyed
       when X-Chat quits, or when the define script is unloaded.
       All other command definitions are saved and reloaded
       when X-Chat starts up again.
    cmd1 and cmd2 MUST NOT include leading slashes.
    Format syntax: %n  Inserts the nth argument (first argument is 0)
           %x-y Inserts the arguments from x to y, inclusive
           %x-  Inserts arguments from x to the end of the input
           %-y  Inserts arguments up to and including y
           \%  A literal % character
    All other text in the format string will be taken literally. Be careful!
    Defined commands are saved in the file definedcommands.txt in the same folder
       as X-Chat. Deleting this file will clear all of your defined commands.
    The UNDEFINE and DEFINEDCMDS commands are also available.
       See /help undefine and /help definedcmds, respectively.

#18543 1 2007-12-24T03:55:55Z jtbandes
I've been pondering this for a while.
This book, apart from simply being a good book, brings up a similar topic.


What is identity? What makes us who we are? How can we be distinguished from any other person, or even any other object? What makes an object what it is?

Surely one might think, as I did after listening to the above series, that what makes an individual being what they are is really their memory. If one loses their memory, all internal sense of identity is gone.

However, if a perfect clone of a person were made, and with a different memory and thus a different internal sense of identity, we might argue that it is not the "same" person, though others might perceive them as being "the same". Similarly, if a completely "different" person was given the exact same memory, we would also say that it is not the "same" person, though that person would internally have the same sense of identity.

Organic objects (and other things as well) are constantly changing. Humans, for instance, are constantly growing new skin cells and sloughing off old ones. Now imagine some sort of object which gradually had all of its comprising particles replaced with similar ones. In our minds, it would still be the "same" object. But, in fact, it would have none of the exact same atoms, molecules, or other types of particles. Where does our sense of "sameness" come from? What makes objects unique, and how do they maintain an identity?

Or is all "identity" and "uniqueness" just something humans have fabricated to explain the universe as we see it?


Discuss.

#20525 1 2008-06-14T17:35:54Z jtbandes
I'm making a simple circle collision thing, and I think I've got all the basics there, but the circles act as if they're somewhat soft. Does anyone know how to make them act rigid? I'll attach the zipped SWF. Here's the code:

function doFrame(event:TimerEvent) {
	for each (var ball:Ball in balls) {
  if (!ball.dragging) {
  	ball.yvel += GRAVITY * 0.05;
  	
  	ball.x += ball.xvel;
  	ball.y += ball.yvel;
  	
  	if (ball.x - ball.radius < 0) {
    ball.x = ball.radius;
    ball.xvel *= -BOUNCE;
  	} else if (ball.x + ball.radius > stage.stageWidth) {
    ball.x = stage.stageWidth - ball.radius;
    ball.xvel *= -BOUNCE;
  	}
  	if (ball.y - ball.radius < 0) {
    ball.y = ball.radius;
    ball.yvel *= -BOUNCE;
  	} else if (ball.y + ball.radius > stage.stageHeight) {
    ball.y = stage.stageHeight - ball.radius;
    ball.yvel *= -BOUNCE;
    ball.xvel *= FRICTION;
    if (Math.abs(ball.yvel) < 0.5) ball.yvel = 0;
  	}
  }
	}
	for (var b:int = 0; b < balls.length; b++) {
  for (var b2:int = b+1; b2 < balls.length; b2++) {
  	ball = balls[b];
  	var ball2:Ball = balls[b2];
  	
  	var xdif:Number = ball2.x - ball.x;
  	var ydif:Number = ball2.y - ball.y;
  	var hyp:Number = Math.√(Math.pow(xdif,2) + Math.pow(ydif,2));
  	
  	var overlap:Number = ball.radius + ball2.radius - hyp;
  	var cosine:Number = xdif / hyp;
  	var sine:Number = ydif / hyp;
  	
  	const velmult:Number = 0.5; // velocity multiplier (arbitrary)
  	
  	if (hyp != 0 && hyp < ball.radius + ball2.radius) {
    // make sure balls equally share repulsion (keeping dragged ones in place)
    const ball2dragmod = (ball2.dragging ? 0 : (ball.dragging ? 2 : 0.5));
    const balldragmod = (ball.dragging ? 0 : (ball2.dragging ? 2 : 0.5));
    
    ball2.x += ball2dragmod * overlap * cosine;
    ball.x -= balldragmod * overlap * cosine;
    ball2.xvel += ball2dragmod * overlap * cosine * velmult;
    ball.xvel -= balldragmod * overlap * cosine * velmult;
    
    ball2.y += ball2dragmod * overlap * sine;
    ball.y -= balldragmod * overlap * sine;
    ball2.yvel += ball2dragmod * overlap * sine * velmult;
    ball.yvel -= balldragmod * overlap * sine * velmult;
  	}
  }
	}
	event.updateAfterEvent();
}
Attachment (lost): post-14-1213464954.zip
